# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-08-06_TotalExtraction_81813840.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
August 12, 2024  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, 
Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne (joined via Zoom at 6:01 p.m.), Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and 
Jeff White. Absent: Bryan Cook. Also present: Kylie Soden, Deputy City Clerk.  The pledge of 
allegiance was recited. 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda).  Mr. Segrave requested item #15 be removed from the consent 
agenda. Support was received by only one member, Mr. Esquibel, so the item remained on the 
Consent Agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried. Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Collins and Mr. 
Segrave voting “no”. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on July 22, 2024.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a transfer of ownership of retail liquor license 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for 307 Racing Management, Inc. d/b/a 307 Racing, 
3310 Ridge Road, Cheyenne, WY (license currently held by Keg, Inc d/b/a D.T.’s Liquor Mart & 
Tusker Bar).  Public comment was made by: Kyle Ridgeway, President, 307 Racing Management, 
Inc.  Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mayor 
Collins, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Segrave and Mr. White. There being no 
further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the 
public hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee. 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statue § 15-1-402 exist for about 8.80 acres of land located west 
of Converse Avenue and north of Dell Range Blvd. There being no comments or input and purpose 
of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statue § 15-1-402 exist for about .035 acres of land located east 
of US 30 and north of and adjacent to E. Pershing Blvd. Public comment was made by: James 
O’Connor, landowner. Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: 
Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins and Mr. Johnson. There being no further comments or input and 
purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 

 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located west of Converse Avenue and north of Dell Range Boulevard – referred to Public Services 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classification of AG Agricultural for land annexed to the City 
of Cheyenne located west of Converse Avenue and north of Dell Range Boulevard – referred to 
Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from AG Agricultural to NR-3 Neighborhood 
Residential - High Density for land located west of Converse Avenue and north of Dell Range 
Boulevard – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located east of US-30 and north of and adjacent to East Pershing Boulevard – referred to Public 
Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
            ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classification of MUR Mixed-Use Residential for land annexed 
to the City of Cheyenne located east of US-30 and north of and adjacent to East Pershing Boulevard 
– referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from LI Light Industrial to CB Community Business 
for land located northeast of the intersection of Hutchins Drive and Industry Drive – referred to 
Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from LR Low-Density Residential to CB Community 
Business for land located at 3501 Forest Drive (located southeast of the intersection of Pershing 
Boulevard and Forest Drive) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Section 2.76.040, Designation of Downtown 
Development District, of Chapter 2.76, Downtown Development Authority, of Title 2, 
Administration and Personnel, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to 
expand the Downtown Development District – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsors 
– Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Seagrave, and Mr. White)    
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Supporting the Clean Crow Creek Initiative on behalf of the Governing 
Body for the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6420) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Amending the City of Cheyenne 2023 Program Year Annual Action 
Plan, prepared by the Cheyenne Housing & Community Development Office.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#6421) 
 

[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of an application to the Wyoming State Loan 
and Investment Board for a loan from the Wyoming Drinking Water State Revolving Fund for the 
Hypochlorite Generation Project in the amount of four million dollars and authorizing the Director 
of the Board of Public Utilities to execute the application and all related documents. Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#6422) 
 
[CA] 
Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Office of Youth Alternatives, and Laramie County 
to provide probation/diversion services to youth appearing in both the Laramie County Circuit 
Court and the Municipal Court for the City of Cheyenne (Revenue to the City). Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#8025) 
 
[CA] 
Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Office of Youth Alternatives, and Laramie County 
to provide services to the Laramie County Juvenile Crisis Shelter (Revenue to the City). Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. (#8026) 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Lotus Engineering & 
Sustainability, LLC, for the Comprehensive Climate Action Plan (CCAP) Preparation for 
Cheyenne, Wyoming (EPA Climate Pollution Reduction Grant (CPRG)) – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Office of Youth Alternatives, and the Laramie 
County Community Juvenile Services Joint Powers Board for case management services and 
substance abuse prevention and evaluation (Revenue to the City) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Grant Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the US Endowment for Forestry and 
Communities for a National Forests Grant for protecting source water and reducing wildfire risk 
for rural communities (US Endowment for Forestry and Communities Grant) – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Grant Agreement between the City of Cheyenne Metropolitan Planning Organization 
(MPO) and the US Department of Transportation Federal Highway Administration to develop a 
Comprehensive Safety Action Plan (Federal Highway Administration SS4A Grant and 2021 
Specific Purpose Option Tax Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
    
[CA] 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7975 between the City of Cheyenne and TPT Trails 
for additional design-build services and trail maintenance for the Belvoir Ranch, Phase 2 Design-
Build Trail Project (Belvoir Recreation Fund) – referred to Finance Committee.   
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-18-24 for SCBA (Self Contained Breathing Apparatus) 
Compressors for Cheyenne Fire Rescue (Homeland Security, Assistance to Firefighters Grant). 
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $399,829.08).   
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-29-24 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Miller 
and Sons Construction, Inc. for the Vandehei Repair and Restoration Project (General Fund 
Reserves). Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $180,652.10). 
(#8027)  

 
Appointment of Robert R. Rose as Municipal Court Magistrate Hearing Judge. Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Esquibel. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote (Mayoral 
appointments). 
 
 
No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
Notice of Emergency Procurement was given. In accordance with the City of Cheyenne 
Purchasing Policy, an Emergency Procurement has been authorized by Mayor Collins for the Civic 
Center Roof Drain Failure Repair.  The emergency repair has been authorized to Master 
Mechanical, Inc. for a total of $100,000.00 (2019-2022 Optional 1% Sales Tax Fund and General 
Fund).  Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, 
Mayor Collins, Mr. Johnson and Mr. Roybal.  
 
The following members of the governing body made comments under Other Business: Dr. 
Aldrich, Mr. Johnson and Mr. Segrave.  
 
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:55 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kylie Soden, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Deputy City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
August 21, 2024 
 
 
 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-08-06_TotalExtraction_43805216.pdf
   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
August 12, 2024 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in special session on this date beginning 
at 7:01 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting, for the purpose of 
collective labor bargaining with the Cheyenne Professional Firefighters, International Association 
of Firefighters, Local No. 279.  Present were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS 
– Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne (via 
Zoom), Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White.  Absent: Bryan Cook.  Also present: Kylie 
Soden, Deputy City Clerk, Amber Ash, Chief of Staff, Stefanie Boster, City Attorney, John Brodie, 
Deputy City Attorney, Robin Lockman, City Treasurer and Chief John Kopper, Cheyenne Fire 
Rescue.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
  
Mayor Collins advised the special session was for the purpose of re-negotiating the existing 
contract labor agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Fire Union. He briefly provided 
details relating to the proposed Ground Rules for the special session and the Ground Rules were 
agreed upon and signed by the City and Union. Vincent McQueeney, spokesperson for the 
Cheyenne Professional Firefighters, International Association of Firefighters, Local No. 279, 
expressed appreciation to the City for their continued collaboration in working with the Union to 
address and update the contract. Mayor Collins advised the only sections that would be opened for 
re-negotiation are Section 2 – Definitions, Section 4 – Terms of Agreement, Section 13 – 
Management Rights, Section 15 – Department Staffing, Section 21 – Classification of 
Employment and Basic Wages and Section 27 – Sick Leave.  Mayor Collins provided information 
on the City’s and Union’s requested changes for each section of the contract which include: Section 
2 – Definitions – acceptable fire-training academy, Cadre/Training Instructor, Ladder Company 
and Squad/Support Company; Section 4 – Terms of Agreement – update term date; Section 13 – 
Management Rights – additional management right to address organizational risk; Section 15 – 
Staffing – update department staffing level to 100, increase how many firefighters are assigned to 
each shift to 29, remove Front Range Fire Academy, increase staffing of Suppression, Community 
Risk Reduction and Support and Administration divisions to 98; Section 21 – Classification of 
Employment and Basic Wages – update to reflect current compensation program, table updated to 
reflect Cost-of-Living Adjustment and Section 27 – Sick Leave – update to reflect proper 
conversion requirements outlined in Section 24 for sick leave for daytime personnel.  The City and 
Union announced acceptance of the proposal.  Mayor Collins noted the revised contract would 
come before the governing body at the next City Council meeting.    
 
  
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:13 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 

   
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kylie Soden, Deputy City Clerk  
 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
Wednesday, August 21, 2024  
 
 
 
 


--- END OF FILE ---

STAFF REPORT
FILE:
PUDC-24-96: Ridge View Apartments Phase 2 - Annexation
REQUEST:
An annexation of 8.80 acres of land
LOCATION:
West of Converse Avenue and North of Dell Range Boulevard.
APPLICANT:
Kelly Hafner
AGENT:
Kelly Hafner
OWNERS: 
Kipling Sheppard and WPP Thomas Ranch LLC
PURPOSE:
For multifamily development within City Limits. 
PREPARED BY:
Connor White, Planner II
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-24-96 
 
 
August 12, 2024 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting annexing 8.80 acres of land to allow future development within City Limits. The 
land is currently vacant. The annexed area will be assigned a City zone based on the underlying County zone. 
The applicant has requested to rezone the property to NR-3 Neighborhood Residential – High Density from 
the City assigned zone.  
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. The petition to annex has been accompanied by an assigned 
City zone of AG Agricultural.   
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the future 
occupants. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to City Fire and Police and 
sanitation services. The property, if occupied, will also be required to be connected to City water 
and sanitary sewer instead of having wells and septic systems.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)]

PUDC-24-96 
 
 
August 12, 2024 
This property, once developed, will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part 
of the city. The property is adjacent to the City to the east. The contiguity to the City and the 
proximity to surrounding urban-style development makes the parcel a natural geographic part 
of the City. The development of this property will benefit those who live within the City and will 
be a part of the social and economic fabric that is Cheyenne.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. Utilities exist in the area and will be expanded into the 
proposed area as it develops. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation is about 33% contiguous, along the eastern edge.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on July 9, 2024. The public 
hearing for this annexation is scheduled for August 12, 2024. Twenty (20) business days (on the 
City’s calendar) prior to the announced public hearing is July 15, 2024. The earliest confirmation 
of delivery was dated July 10, 2024. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
 
 

PUDC-24-96 
 
 
August 12, 2024 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with urban residential uses. These areas are expected to develop with full access 
to urban services – such areas are best served by being within City limits. The area is within the Urban 
Service Boundary. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
most services typically available to urban areas are available to be extended to this property.  
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Submitted Application 
4. Applicant-submitted annexation map

districts established on land or property currently without a City zone assignment including land

recommended approval of said zoning assignment.

assignment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.8.d of 
the UDC.

classifications of the real property as described in Section 1 is hereby established as AG 
Agricultural.

the Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map

Section 8 below.

publication, and upon approval and publication of the affiliated annexation.

PUDC-24-96 
 
 
August 5, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve the assigned zoning for land west of Converse 
Avenue and north of Dell Range Boulevard to AG Agricultural as shown in the attached zone change 
map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.8 Assigned Zoning 
 
BACKGROUND: 
The applicant has petitioned for land west of Converse Avenue and north of Dell Range Boulevard to be 
annexed into city limits. Annexation of a parcel requires a city zone to be established. Per UDC 2.2.8 a zone 
will be assigned based on the underlying county zone and per the policy document adopted by the city. 
Should the applicant wish to have a different zone at the time of annexation, they will be required to submit 
a subsequent zoning map amendment, proposing a different zone. The applicant is requesting a different City 
zone. The applicant is requesting a zone change to NR3-Nieghborhood Residential – High Density.  
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The assigned zone is the most analogous City zone to the current zone when considering 
allowed uses. Zoning for standard County zones shall be assigned based on the policy 
document, while zones not listed on the policy document and terminated City zones shall be 
assigned a City zone on a case-by-case basis. (UDC 2.2.8.d.1) 
 
The underlying county zone is A-1 Agricultural and Rural Residential. The most analogous city 
zone is AG Agricultural according to the policy document. The Assigned Zoning policy document 
is attached to the staff report as attachment number two.  
 
Establishment of the City zone matches the policy document; therefore, the item complies with 
review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, there were no comments.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.8.(d). 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning subject to conditions or modifications.  

PUDC-24-96 
 
 
August 5, 2024 
3. Recommend denial of the Assigned Zoning based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the Assigned Zoning until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or recommends conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for their recommendation. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the assigned zoning for land west of Converse Avenue and north of Dell 
Range Boulevard to AG Agricultural as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project 
meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
 
1. Zoning Map 
2. Assigned Zoning Policy Document 
3. Use Differences

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM 
AG 
AGRICULTURAL 
TO 
NR-3 
NEIGHBORHOOD 
RESIDENTIAL – HIGH DENSITY FOR LAND LOCATED WEST OF 
CONVERSE AVENUE AND NORTH OF DELL RANGE BOULEVARD." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code ("UDC"). 
 
 
Section 2. That on August 5, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zoning map amendment that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zoning map amendment. 
 
 
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning map 
amendment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of 
the UDC. 
 
 
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as Lot 1, Block One, Scenic Development, 5th Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby 
changed to NR-3 Neighborhood Residential – High Density.  
 
 
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 7 below. 
 
 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication of this ordinance and recording of the associated Plat.

Kelly Hafner

Connor White, Planner II  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-24-97 
 
 
August 5, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lot 1, Block 1, Scenic Development, 5th Filing to NR-3 Neighborhood 
Residential – High Density as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the 
review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
x 
UDC Article 5 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from AG Agricultural to NR-3 Neighborhood 
Residential – High Density for roughly 8.8 acres of land. The land is currently vacant. The land is currently in 
the County and is being annexed. The assigned zoning with the annexation is AG. There is an associated plat 
with this zone change which would create one parcel and dedicate additional right-of-way for multifamily 
development. The plat application is PUDC-24-107.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure.  
 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property and the surrounding area is Urban Residential 
and Mixed-use Commercial. According to the comprehensive plan the optimal City zones for the 
 
Future Land Use Designation

annexed)

UDC-24-97 
 
 
August 5, 2024 
subject property’s future land use designation is any one of the residential zones (for the Urban 
Residential area), including NR-3; and MUB for the Mixed-use Commercial area. However, the 
NR-3 zone is essentially the residential portion of the MUB zone, and other commercial 
development is in the area. 
 
The NR-3 zone is intended to develop with high-density housing. The comprehensive plan 
encourages high density housing to locate…   
 
Based on the policies and future land use map, NR-3 zoning is appropriate at this location.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
The proposed NR3 zone is to match the existing NR3 zone to the east to allow the continuation 
of the existing multifamily development. This area is planned to be residential and a mix of 
commercial according to the Comprehensive Plan. The currently vacant land surrounding the 
development is anticipated to develop with a mix of commercial and higher-density residential 
uses. The higher-density residential uses associated with the NR-3 zone would be compatible 
with this anticipated development pattern. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The property is being annexed into City Limits and being assigned the AG Agricultural zone. The 
AG zone may be appropriate if the landowner wished to continue the current agricultural use, 
but is not appropriate for urban development. The AG zone would not allow multifamily 
development. The NR3 would match the existing multifamily development to the east and 
allows multifamily as a use by right.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The proposed area is also being replatted and proposing to extend E Carlson St. Utilities and 
infrastructure will be extended with the development. The proposed development is an 
extension of the existing one to the east. The NR-3 zone is intended to have a more compact 
development pattern than the current block allows. Connections to the street network to the 
north, west, and south are needed, as are internal pedestrian connections if road connections 
are infeasible. The street network issues will be addressed with the proposed platting action. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 

UDC-24-97 
 
 
August 5, 2024 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The NR-3 zone is intended primarily for high-density, urban residential uses with consistent 
neighborhood character. All uses require full access to public infrastructure and city services. 
The regulations are designed to protect the neighborhood character of the district and protect 
the primarily residential use of land. The overall density of this district should be more than 15 
dwelling units per acre. The proposed project is multifamily development which will likely 
achieve these objectives and match the existing development to the east.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
 
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The NR-3 zone allows a variety of residential uses. The applicant intends to build a continuation 
of their existing multifamily development, which was recently constructed and managed to 
meet current design standards. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No major comments received in regard to the proposed zone.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
x 
Dell Range Boulevard Corridor Study Phase II – Section 20  
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.   
The Dell Range Boulevard Corridor Study Phase II – Section 20 plan largely considered land uses 
consistent with the comprehensive plan for this area. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 

UDC-24-97 
 
 
August 5, 2024 
Through the review process, review comments were received expressing the appropriateness of the  
NR-3 zone at this location. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The NR-3 zone 
is appropriate based on the future land use map, surrounding zoning, surrounding development 
patterns, and proposed development. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from AG to NR-3. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map 
5. Differences in uses per zone

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE ANNEXING TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING, LAND LOCATED EAST OF US-30 AND NORTH OF AND 
ADJACENT TO EAST PERSHING BOULEVARD." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the City of Cheyenne hereby finds as follows: 
 
(a) 
James O’Connor, owner of:  
 
Lots 33 and 34, Sunnyside Addition, 4th Filing, Resub TR 137-140 
 
have petitioned the governing body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real 
property described herein and to make it a part thereof; 
 
(b) 
That an accurate annexation map of said land is attached to this ordinance as 
Exhibit A, said map prepared by Jeffrey B. Jones, a Professional Land Surveyor, 
licensed in the State of Wyoming, PLS Number 5910; 
 
(c) 
That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; 
 
(d) 
That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; 
 
(e) 
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and 
other services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, 
be furnished; 
 
(f) 
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned A-1 
Agricultural and Rural Residential by the Laramie County Commissioners; and 
 
(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and 
its governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area 
pursuant to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the 
entire annexed area. 
 
Section 2.  That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
 
Section 3.  That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for 
7:04 pm, Aug 05 2024

Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to 
the official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Section 4.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared 
illegal or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the 
remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 5.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

STAFF REPORT
FILE:
PUDC-24-102: 4614 E Pershing Blvd. - Annexation
REQUEST:
An annexation of 0.35 acres of land
LOCATION:
East of US-30 and north of and adjacent to E Pershing Blvd.
APPLICANT:
Jack Studley
AGENT:
Jack Studley
OWNERS: 
James O’Connor
PURPOSE:
Connect to City water.
PREPARED BY:
Connor White, Planner II
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-24-102 
 
 
August 12, 2024 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting annexing 0.35 acres of land to allow a City water connection. The land is currently 
used as a single-family home. The annexed area will be assigned a City zone based on the underlying County 
zone. No other applications have been submitted for this property at this time.  
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. The petition to annex has been accompanied by an assigned 
City zone of MUR Mixed-use Residential zone.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the future 
occupants. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to City Fire and Police and 
sanitation services. By being within City Limits the property will be able to connect to City 
services.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
 
This property is developed with a single-family home on a parcel smaller than those currently 
allowed by the County, this is urban development that already is a natural, geographic, 
economic, and social part of the city. The property is adjacent to the City to the west. The

PUDC-24-102 
 
 
August 12, 2024 
contiguity to the City and the proximity to surrounding urban-style development makes the 
parcel a natural geographic part of the City. The annexation of this property will benefit those 
who live within the City and will be a part of the social and economic fabric that is Cheyenne.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. Most utilities are within the area. Sanitary sewer would need 
to be extended to serve this property.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation is about 24% contiguous, along the southern edge.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on July 9, 2024. The public 
hearing for this annexation is scheduled for August 12, 2024. Twenty (20) business days (on the 
City’s calendar) prior to the announced public hearing is July 15, 2024. The earliest confirmation 
of delivery was dated July 10, 2024. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
 
 
 
 

PUDC-24-102 
 
 
August 12, 2024 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with urban residential uses. These areas are expected to develop with full access 
to urban services – such areas are best served by being within City limits. The area is within the Urban 
Service Boundary. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process the Board of public utilities did make the following comment: 
1. There is no Sanitary Sewer infrastructure immediately adjacent to the site. Sanitary Sewer 
service will require extension of public infrastructure to serve the site. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
most services typically available to urban areas are available to be extended to this property.  
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Submitted Application 
4. Applicant-submitted annexation map 

.

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY 
OF 
CHEYENNE 
ESTABLISHING 
THE 
ZONING 
CLASSIFICATION OF MUR MIXED-USE RESIDENTIAL FOR LAND 
ANNEXED TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE LOCATED EAST OF US-30 
AND NORTH OF AND ADJACENT TO EAST PERSHING BOULEVARD." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  James O’Connor, owner of: 
 
Lots 33 and 34, Sunnyside Addition, 4th Filing, Resub TR 137-140 
 
have petitioned the governing body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real property 
described herein and to make it a part thereof. 
 
 
Section 2.  That Section 2.2.8 of the UDC provides standards and procedures for zoning 
districts established on land or property currently without a City zone assignment including land 
being annexed to the city.  
 
 
Section 3.  That on August 5, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
public hearings, reviewed the proposed zoning assignment that is the subject of this ordinance, and 
recommended approval of said zoning assignment.  
 
 
Section 4.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning 
assignment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.8.d of 
the UDC. 
 
 
Section 5.  That in accordance with Section 2.2.8, Assigned Zoning, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the zoning 
classifications of the real property as described in Section 1 is hereby established as MUR Mixed-
use Residential. 
 
 
Section 6.   That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend 
the Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 5 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 8 below. 
 
 
Section 7.   Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
7:06 pm, Aug 05 2024

 
Section 8.   That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication, and upon approval and publication of the affiliated annexation.

(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-24-102 
 
 
August 5, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve the assigned zoning for land east of US-30 and north 
of and adjacent to E Pershing Blvd. to MUR Mixed-use Residential as shown in the attached zone change 
map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.8 Assigned Zoning 
 
BACKGROUND: 
The applicant has petitioned for land east of US-30 and north of and adjacent to E Pershing Blvd. to be 
annexed into city limits. Annexation of a parcel requires a city zone to be established. Per UDC 2.2.8 a zone 
will be assigned based on the underlying county zone and per the policy document adopted by the city. 
Should the applicant wish to have a different zone at the time of annexation, they will be required to submit 
a subsequent zoning map amendment, proposing a different zone. The applicant is not requesting a different 
City zone at this time. 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The assigned zone is the most analogous City zone to the current zone when considering 
allowed uses. Zoning for standard County zones shall be assigned based on the policy 
document, while zones not listed on the policy document and terminated City zones shall be 
assigned a City zone on a case-by-case basis. (UDC 2.2.8.d.1) 
 
The underlying county zone is MU Mixed-use. The most analogous city zone is MUR Mixed-use 
Residential according to the policy document. The Assigned Zoning policy document is attached 
to the staff report as attachment number two.  
 
Establishment of the City zone matches the policy document; therefore, the item complies with 
review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, there were no comments.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.8.(d). 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning subject to conditions or modifications.  

PUDC-24-102 
 
 
August 5, 2024 
3. Recommend denial of the Assigned Zoning based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the Assigned Zoning until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or recommends conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for their recommendation. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the assigned zoning for land east of US-30 and north of and adjacent to E 
Pershing Blvd. to MUR Mixed-use Residential as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that 
the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
 
1. Zoning Map 
2. Assigned Zoning Policy Document 
3. Use Differences

WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a

Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”).

ordinance, and recommended approval of said zoning map amendment.

amendment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of 
the UDC.

aforementioned application is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as  Lot 1, Block 1 Wyodigs Properties Subdivision, Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby 
changed to CB Community Business.

August 5, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lot 1, Block 1 Wyodigs Properties Subdivision to CB Community 
Business as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria 
for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
x 
UDC Article 5 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from LI Light Industrial to CB Community Business 
for roughly 3.96 acres of land. The land is currently vacant. The proposed development is a Tractor Supply 
and they would like to have a dog wash facility as part of their business which is not permitted within the LI 
zone.   
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure.  
 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Mixed-use Employment and the 
surrounding area is largely Mixed-use Employment. Further to the south and west the land is 
designated Community Business and further to the north the land is designated Industrial. The

Mixed-use Employment designation is a transition from Community Business to Industrial in this 
area, and a mix of lighter industrial and commercial uses are appropriate. According to the 
comprehensive plan the optimal City zones for the subject property’s future land use 
designation is Mixed-use Employment; however the Mixed-use Employment zone is set up for a 
campus-like atmosphere and this area of the community does not have a campus-like industrial 
park. Secondary zones that would be appropriate to the uses anticipated by the comprehensive 
plan would include both CB and LI zoning. 
 
Based on the policies and future land use map, CB zoning is appropriate at this location.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
The proposed CB zone would match commercial zoning in the area. A node of CB zoning is less 
than 1000’ away to the west. The surrounding zoning is LI, which has similar setbacks, and 
height allowances. The LI zone allows for limited commercial uses and prohibits the most 
intense industrial uses. The CB zone would be compatible with surrounding zoning.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The LI zone allows more industrial-style uses and fewer commercial-style uses. The CB zone 
allows more commercial-style uses, but is much more restrictive on industry. These zones have 
some overlap, and some of the existing uses in the area could exist in either zone. These zones 
are both already in the area.  
 
The applicant is requesting a change to CB because they would like to add a dog washing facility 
to their proposed development. All other uses for the development are permitted within the 
existing LI zone.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The zone change is occurring in a pre-existing neighborhood. No changes to the street network 
are proposed. Accesses will not be changed with the zone change. Infrastructure (streets and 
utilities) exist in the area. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 

UDC-24-121 
 
 
August 5, 2024 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The CB zone is intended for larger commercial uses serving the community as a whole and is 
applicable in activity centers or other areas where regional-serving commercial uses are 
anticipated. Internal circulation between adjacent sites is encouraged. The CB zone applies 
commercial design standards. 
 
This property is near to other CB zoned parcels, and is in an area with ready access to regional 
transportation routes. The proposed facility is a regional-serving commercial facility. The 
proposal meets the intent and applicability of the CB zone.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The CB zone allows a variety of Commercial Uses and some Limited Industrial uses. Should the 
proposed development not move forward, another commercial development could use the 
property.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No major comments received in regard to the proposed zone.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, review comments were received expressing the appropriateness of the  
CB zone at this location. 

UDC-24-121 
 
 
August 5, 2024 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The CB zone is 
appropriate based on the future land use map, surrounding zoning, surrounding development patterns, 
and proposed development. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from LI to CB. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map 
5. Differences in uses per zone

MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Athen Mores, Planner I 
Subject: PUDC-24-119 / 3501 Forest Drive / Zone Change  
Date: 
August 12, 2024 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a Zone Change. This is approved by Resolution.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Ordinance 1st Reading: Monday, August 12, 2024, 6:00pm 
• 
Public Service Committee: Tuesday, August 20, 2024, Noon 
• 
Ordinance 2nd Reading: Monday, August 26, 2024, 6:00pm 
• 
Public Service Committee: Wednesday, September 4, 2024, Noon 
• 
Ordinance 3rd Reading: Monday, September 9, 2024, 6:00pm 
 
Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their August 5, 2024 meeting and unanimously voted to 
recommend the Governing Body deny this application.  The Planning Commission recommended denial 
due to not meeting review criteria 1, 2, and 8.  The Planning Commissions’ analysis was that the project 
did not meet the comprehensive plan, was not compatible with the neighborhood, and presented public 
health, safety, or welfare concerns due to potential traffic impacts from potential commercial 
redevelopment.   
 
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached.

2. Planning Commission Staff Report

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM LR LOW-DENSITY RESIDENTIAL TO CB COMMUNITY 
BUSINESS FOR LAND LOCATED AT 3501 FOREST DRIVE (LOCATED 
SOUTHEAST OF THE INTERSECTION OF PERSHING BOULEVARD 
AND FOREST DRIVE)." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code ("UDC"). 
 
 
Section 2.  That on August 5, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zoning map amendment that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and made a recommendation on said zoning map amendment. 
 
 
Section 3.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning map 
amendment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of 
the UDC. 
 
 
Section 4.  That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved, and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as Block 44, less the east 2’, Mountview Park, 7th Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby 
changed to CB Community Business. 
 
 
Section 5.  That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 7 below. 
 
 
Section 6.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 7.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
August 5, 2024 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
PUDC-24-119: a portion of Block 44Mountview Park, 7th Filing – Zoning 
Map Amendment (zone change)  
REQUEST: 
A request to rezone approximately 5.17 acres of land from  
LR-Low Density Residential to CB-Community Business. 
LOCATION: 
3501 Forest Drive, Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
APPLICANT: 
Robert Born 
AGENT: 
William Lewis 
OWNER: 
Robert Born 
ZONING: 
MR Medium-density Residential 
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Urban Residential 
PURPOSE: 
Zone change to allow a new Electronic Message Center sign 
PREPARED BY: 
Athen Mores, Planner I  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

UDC-24-119 
August 5, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
Move to approve the rezoning of All Block 44, Less the East 2', Mountview Park, 7th filing to CB- 
Community Business as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the 
review criteria for approval. 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
•
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment
•
UDC Article 5
•
PlanCheyenne
BACKGROUND: 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from MR Medium-Density Residential to CB- 
Community Business for roughly 5.17 acres of land. The land is currently being used as a church, and they 
would like to install an LED Electronic Message Center sign into the existing brick signage.   
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
1)
The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1)
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with
existing infrastructure.
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Urban Residential and the surrounding area
is Urban Residential, Community Business, and Public. According to the comprehensive plan the
optimal City zones for the subject property’s future land use is one of the many residential zone
Future Land Use Designation

UDC-24-119 
 
 
August 5, 2024 
districts, however, on the south and east, the future land use plan’s optimal zone is CB 
Community Business. Except in cases of a hard barrier (such as a railroad or interstate, the 
future land use map should be viewed as using ‘fuzzy’ lines, that is, areas should gradually 
transition from one future land use to another.  
 
When the future land use map was created, many previously-developed areas were given future 
land use designations based on current zoning or current ownership. Areas exempt from 
property taxes were given the ‘public quasi-public’ future land use designation, even if they 
were private entities. This resulted in HUD homes in the middle of neighborhoods being 
designated ‘public’ despite being surrounded by the Urban Residential category. Churches also 
received this designation, including the subject property. With the Connect 2045 comprehensive 
plan update, any privately-owned ‘public’-designated parcel was assigned a new future land use 
designation based on existing zoning. As the subject parcel was zoned residential, it was given 
the ‘Urban Residential’ future land use designation. 
 
The current use of the parcel is nonconforming (prohibited) in the LR zone and would require 
commercial zoning by the current zoning code. Any expansion of the use would require a zone 
change or conditional use approval.  
 
Based on the policies and future land use map, CB zoning is appropriate for this location.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
Surrounding properties are zoned CB, P, and LR. The LR zoneis described as low density 
residential. The P zone allows a variety of public uses, which are all non-residential in nature..   
The CB zone is to the east and south, and is the requested zone for this parcel. Generally, the 
code encourages buffering between the CB and LR zones. 
 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The current use of the parcel is nonconforming (prohibited) in the LR zone and would require 
commercial zoning by the current zoning code. Any expansion of the use would require a zone 
change or conditional use approval.  
The LR, as put forward in the UDC, is intended for larger-block, front-loaded, suburban style 
development patterns.  CB zoning is intended for Retail, Employment, Service and Civic uses that 
may serve a number of residential neighborhoods or other districts in meeting daily needs, 
discretionary needs, or other business functions.  The current function of the property is better 
suited to the CB zone, such as the other business to the east. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 

UDC-24-119 
 
 
August 5, 2024 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The zone change is occurring in a pre-existing neighborhood. No changes to the street network 
are proposed. Accesses will not be changed with the zone change. Infrastructure (streets and 
utilities) exist in the area. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The CB District is intended for Retail, Employment, Service and Civic uses that may serve number 
of residential neighborhoods or other districts in meeting daily needs, discretionary needs, or 
other business functions.  The current function of the property is better suited to the CB zone, 
such as the other business to the east. Due to the nature and users of CB-zoned property, the CB 
zone has a much more flexible signage allowance. The impetus behind the request is to allow 
the current site more signage options. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The CB zone allows a variety of commercial uses including retail, employment, service, and civic 
uses. The applicant intends to install a new LED sign into the existing brick signage of the church.  
Changing from LR zone to CB zone would enable this. Should the existing use cease and the 
property need redevelopment, the options presented by the CB zone are more logical and 
feasible than those of the LR zone. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
Review comments discussed how the proposal meets comprehensive plan principles. The parcel 
should be this zone based on the physical form of the area to the north, east and southeast, and 
the neighboring business. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 

UDC-24-119 
 
 
August 5, 2024 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, review comments were received expressing the appropriateness of the  
CB zone at this location. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The CB zone is 
appropriate based on the future land use map, surrounding zoning, surrounding development patterns, 
and proposed development. 
 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from LR to CB. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map 
5. Differences in uses per zone

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-08-06_TotalExtraction_34595398.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Seth Lloyd, Senior Planner 
Subject: PUDC-24-119 / 3501 Forest Drive / Zone Change  
Date: 
August 21, 2024 
 
At Public Services Committee on August 20, 2024, there was discussion on the zone change request at 
Pershing and Forest. The impetus behind the zone change request was the applicant’s desire for a 
larger-than-allowed electronic message center (EMC). There was some discussion on whether or not the 
zone change was the correct way to process this request. Public testimony was received expressing 
concerns about both the proposed sign and the proposed zoning. 
 
Planning Commission 
The Planning Commission was generally sympathetic to the signage request, but due to the potential 
impacts of redevelopment on the current neighborhood to the west, recommended denial of the zone 
change. Public testimony at the planning commission expressed concerns about both the proposed sign 
and the proposed zoning. Some of those commenting were against any increase to signage allowances, 
but there were a few who appeared to accept the signage increase, just not the zone change proposal. 
 
Staff Recommendations 
Due to the location of the property, current use, and redevelopment options, Staff recommended 
approval of the zone change. Should the Governing Body wish to approve an increase to signage 
allowances without approving the zone change, Staff recommends the Governing Body direct staff to 
craft a text amendment to allow for larger signage for all similarly situated properties. 
 
Background 
EMC’s are any portion of a sign that is changed via digital methods, examples include signs like those 
found at Applebee’s, Lincolnway Pawn, and Cheyenne Little Player’s Theater. These signs are currently 
allowed in residential zones for certain non-residential uses such as schools, churches, and theaters (see 
UDC 6.5.4.c.1). However, these are only allowed at 40% of the allowed sign area, and in most residential 
zones the allowed sign area for these uses is 48sqft (therefore 19sqft for an EMC).  
 
For a non-residential property owner wanting a larger-than-19sqft EMC in a residential zone, the 
following options could allow a larger EMC:  zone change, variance, administrative adjustment, and code 
amendment. Attached are a list of some of the pros and cons of the various options. 
 
Attachments: 
1. Comparison of Processes Resulting in an Increase in Signage Allowances  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

Comparison of Processes Resulting in an Increase in Signage Allowances 
 
Zone Change 
Pros: 
• 
Recognizes a property is suitable for higher-intensity uses and therefore higher-intensity signage 
• 
Gives more options for redevelopment 
• 
It makes it easier to expand a non-residential facility 
Cons: 
• 
Not all sites are suitable for rezoning 
• 
Sites close to neighborhoods may not cleanly and clearly meet all review criteria 
• 
Redevelopment near a neighborhood could have a significant impact on the neighborhood 
 
Variance 
Pros: 
• 
Applies to properties on a case-by-case basis 
• 
Recognizes uniqueness of the property (such as being adjacent to a major street) 
• 
Does not allow overly-impactful development into (or near to) a residential neighborhood 
Cons: 
• 
Variances are a ‘high bar’ to clear – an applicant must prove: 
o Unique property conditions 
o True hardship (not just convenience)   
o The relief is the minimum necessary 
o The relief is in harmony with the intent of the code 
• 
Potential for consistency issues if only occasionally granted 
The best practice in land use law is to change the code if variances are being granted for ‘common 
sense’ purposes instead of true variance criteria purposes. 
 
Administrative Adjustment 
Pros: 
• 
Applies to properties on a case-by-case basis 
• 
Recognizes uniqueness of the property (such as being adjacent to a major street) 
• 
Does not allow overly-impactful development into (or near to) a residential neighborhood 
• 
Does not have nearly as high a bar to clear to receive approval (compared to a variance) 
• 
Allows for ‘common sense’ solutions for circumstances where the proposal is ‘close enough’ to 
the requirement 
Cons: 
• 
Limited in scope – this is meant to be minor relief, not a major allowance change 
• 
Lacks public process  
NOTE: for this request, the desired sign area is beyond of the scope of the administrative adjustment. 
 
Code Amendment 
Pros: 
• 
Applies fairly to all properties in the City  
• 
Allows for a signage increase without the potentially excessive action of a zone change 
• 
Allows for a signage increase without having to clear the ‘high bar’ set for variances 
Cons: 
• 
Application to the entire City may have unintended consequences 
Typically, applicants are not directed to request code amendments so that the code does not become 
overly crafted to specific projects and properties.
