1 
 
SECTION 1.  PARTIES 
 
The parties to this Agreement are the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a municipal 
corporation, hereinafter referred to as the “City,” and the Cheyenne Firefighters, Local Union No. 
279, International Association of Firefighters, hereinafter referred to as the “Union.” 
 

2 
 
SECTION 2.  DEFINITIONS 
Acceptable Fire-Training Academy: 
A school whose program certification requirements meet the professional qualifications and the 
job performance requirements (JPR’s) for Firefighter I, Firefighter II, and Hazardous Materials 
Operations as provided by the National Fire Protection Association (NFPA). 
Bargaining Agent: 
Local Union No. 279 is the agent representing the firefighters for the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
and shall be recognized as the sole and exclusive bargaining agent and shall have the scope of 
authority to bargain for rates of pay, hours of work, and other working conditions. 
Base Pay: 
The agreed rate of pay for a firefighter based on their rank and step as specified in Section 21 
excluding overtime pay, longevity pay, holiday pay, education pay, and specialty pay. 
Cadre/Training Instructor: 
The Cadre/Training Instructor position may be utilized to fulfill the needs of the department during 
recruit academies or to meet the training needs of the Department outside of academy 
opportunities.  Assignments made to this position shall not exceed twelve months in duration.  If 
a Member is retained for time periods outside of an active academy, the Member shall be utilized 
to meet the training demands of Cheyenne Fire Rescue.  An assignment made to fill this position 
shall not violate Section 15, unless otherwise agreed upon between the Fire Chief and the Union. 
Civil Service Commission: 
The City of Cheyenne Fire Department Civil Service Commission. 
 

3 
 
Command Staff: 
The Fire Chief, Deputy Fire Chief.  
Command Officers: 
Battalion Chiefs 
Cross Staffing:  
The functioning of a comprised company to split its personnel to operate specialized apparatus at 
the same scene. Specialized apparatus shall not be utilized at companies where the call concurrency 
rate exceeds fifteen percent. 
Company Officers: 
Lieutenants. 
Day Personnel: 
A Member who works an eight or ten-hour tour of duty. 
Days: 
For any period of time specified in this Agreement, days shall be computed in accordance with the 
Rules and Regulations of the City of Cheyenne Fire Department Civil Service Commission. 
Department: 
The Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department. 
Deputy Fire Chief: 
Assistant to the Fire Chief, acts as Fire Chief in their absence and can satisfy the job qualifications 
outlined in the Civil Service Rules and Regulations in effect on July 1, 2023. 
 
 

4 
 
Dual Assignment:  
The functioning of a comprised company to split its personnel to operate different apparatus, 
separated and on different scenes. 
Eligibility List: 
A list established by the City of Cheyenne Fire Department Civil Service Commission pursuant to 
its Rules and Regulations.  
E.M.S.: 
Shall mean Emergency Medical Services. 
Engine Company: 
A pumping apparatus of at least 750gpm capacity, consisting of a minimum three-person crew that 
is comprised of at least a lieutenant, engineer, and firefighter, of which at least one is a paramedic.  
E.O.D.: 
Shall mean earned off-duty time or compensatory time off. 
 
Extraordinary Circumstances: 
An event or condition which could not reasonably be anticipated or expected to occur and is 
beyond the usual, customary, or regular duties of firefighters. 
Fire Chief: 
The Chief of the Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department and can satisfy the job qualifications outlined 
in the Civil Service Rules and Regulations in effect on July 1, 2023. 
Grievance: 
The procedure for the resolution of disputes arising under this Agreement as specified in Section8.  
 
 

5 
 
Immediate Family: 
 
 
Spouse  
 
 
Grandmother 
 
Daughter 
 
 
Grandfather 
 
Son 
 
 
 
Grandchildren 
 
Mother 
 
 
Mother-in-Law 
 
Father  
 
 
Father-in-Law 
 
Sister  
 
 
Sister-in-Law 
 
Brother 
 
 
Brother-in-Law 
 
Step-Mother  
 
Daughter-in-Law 
 
Step-Father 
 
 
Son-in-Law 
 
Step-Children 
 
Incident Commander: 
The highest-ranking Officer who initially responds to any call for service or who has accepted 
transfer of command. 
Ladder Company: 
An aerial apparatus of at least 75’ with pumping capabilities, consisting of a minimum four-person 
crew that is comprised of at least a lieutenant, two engineers, and a firefighter, of which at least 
one is a paramedic. Notwithstanding these crew staffing requirements, and for the purpose of 
staffing flexibility and/or providing additional staffing resources, a “Ladder Company” may 
consist of a crew that is comprised of one engineer assigned with two firefighters, except this 
staffing level may not eliminate the engineer rank total of seven per shift. 
Members: 
All regularly sworn employees of the Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department, as covered by the 
Collective Labor Agreement (excluding the Fire Chief and Deputy Fire Chief). 
Officer in Charge: 
Any Officer in a Member’s chain of command. 
 

6 
 
On-Call: 
Members may be required to be available for callback for the performance of duties as determined 
by the Fire Chief and/or Deputy Fire Chief, and as compensated in accordance with Section 21, 
Classification of Employment and Basic Wages.  
Platoon Personnel: 
A Member who works a twenty-four-hour tour of duty. 
Rank: 
Any of the ranks specified in the Rules and Regulations of the City of Cheyenne Fire Department 
Civil Service Commission. 
Rescue Company: 
An initial attack pumping apparatus of at least 250gpm capacity, consisting of a minimum three-
person crew that is comprised of a lieutenant, an engineer, and a firefighter, of which at least one 
is a paramedic.  
Rules and Regulations: 
The Rules and Regulations of the City of Cheyenne Fire Department Civil Service Commission. 
Seniority: 
The time of consecutive service a Member has accumulated from the date of the Member’s 
employment and shall not be interpreted as time in grade or rank. 
Set:  
Two consecutive 24-hour shifts, served on the Member’s assigned shift. 
“Shall” and “May”: 
“Shall” is mandatory; “May” is permissive. 

7 
 
Shift: 
The number of hours worked in a tour of duty by: 
A. 
Day Personnel - Personnel assigned to work a forty-hour week (Monday through Friday). 
B. 
Platoon Personnel - Personnel assigned to work twenty-four-hour shifts. 
Shift Trainers 
Personnel who have been assigned to assist Fire Training or EMS.  
Specialty Apparatus: 
Any fire department apparatus or vehicle that is intended for specific use by duty personnel for 
their response. These are not defined Company apparatus and examples include, but not limited to 
brush/wildland apparatus, boats, UTV’s, hazardous materials response vehicles, and high/low 
angle rescue response vehicles. 
Specialty Team 
Cheyenne Fire Rescue personnel assigned to the Hazardous Materials and Specialized Rescue 
Teams.   
Squad/Support Company: 
A rescue apparatus capable of transporting patients, consisting of a minimum two-person crew that 
is comprised at a minimum of a firefighter EMT-P and a firefighter EMT-B. At least one assigned 
member shall be a Firefighter Step 2. Probationary firefighters shall not be included as members 
of the crew. Squads shall not be dispatched to structure fire responses as a single resource.  They 
may be attached/assigned to a Ladder and/or Engine Company and will operate under that 
company officer on the fire ground. 
 

8 
 
Temporary Assignment: 
An assignment to a position and rank for a period of no more than twelve months to meet the needs 
of the Department.  The Department shall not assign more than one Member at any given time.  
Vacancy: 
A vacancy is any open position resulting from resignation, termination, retirement, death, 
promotion, or voluntary or involuntary demotion.

9 
 
SECTION 3.  EXCLUSIVE BARGAINING AGENT 
 
The paid Members of the Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department, the regularly constituted Fire 
Department of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, by a majority vote, have selected the I.A.F.F. 
Local Union No. 279 as the sole and exclusive bargaining agent for all of the Members of the 
Department, except the Fire Chief and the Deputy Fire Chief. 
 
The Union and the City’s obligation to negotiate is subject to Wyo. Stat. §§ 27-10-101 
through 27-10-109. 
 
The City shall not enter into any other agreement with Members serving as firefighters, 
individually or collectively, or with any other organization, which in any way conflicts with the 
provisions of this Collective Labor Agreement. 
 

10 
 
SECTION 4.  TERM OF AGREEMENT 
 
This Agreement shall become effective 12:00 a.m., August 27, 2024, and remain in effect 
until 11:59 p.m., June 30, 2025. 

11 
 
SECTION 5.  SEPARABILITY 
 
If any clause, sentence, paragraph, or part of this Agreement, or the application thereof to 
any person or circumstance, shall for any reason be adjudged by a court of competent jurisdiction 
to be invalid, such judgment shall not affect, impair, or invalidate the remainder of the Agreement. 

12 
 
SECTION 6.  EMBODIMENT OF AGREEMENT 
 
This Collective Labor Agreement constitutes the sole and complete agreement between the 
parties and embodies all the terms and conditions governing the wages, rates of pay, and working 
conditions of the Members represented by the Union.   All costs associated with the arbitration 
shall be shared equally by the City and the Union.  The parties acknowledge they have had the 
opportunity to present and discuss any subject germane to collective bargaining.  Any prior 
commitment or agreement between the City and the Union or any individual Member covered by 
this Agreement that has been negotiated by the Union is hereby superseded. 
 

13 
 
SECTION 7.  RE-NEGOTIATIONS 
 
At any time prior to the expiration date of this Agreement, any provision herein may be re-
negotiated by mutual agreement of the City and the Union.  No part of this Agreement shall be 
opened until mutual agreement is reduced in writing and signed by both parties, consisting of the 
bargaining committee for the Union and the Governing Body of the City.  Only the issue(s) in 
question shall be re-negotiated, and not the entire Agreement. 
 

14 
 
SECTION 8.  GRIEVANCE PROCEDURES 
 
A grievance is defined as a dispute or difference of opinion between the City and a 
Member, Members, or the Union over the meaning, interpretation, application, or the inequitable 
application of an expressed provision of the Agreement.  Any Member may file a grievance under 
this Agreement protesting the exercise of Management Rights, but to prevail, the Member must 
establish a violation by the City of the specific terms of this Agreement.  Any grievance filed 
against the City shall be in writing and if a Member’s grievance, it shall contain the name and 
position of the affected Member(s).  The grievance shall contain a clear and concise statement of 
the grievance, the issue or issues involved, the relief sought, the date the incident or violation took 
place, and the specific section or sections alleged to have been violated. 
 
For a grievance to be considered timely, the Union’s Grievance Committee must submit it 
in writing within ten business days of the date the grievant knew of the event giving rise to the 
dispute, following the steps below: 
Step 1 
 
The Union’s Grievance Committee may submit the grievance in writing to the Deputy Fire 
Chief who will then schedule a meeting with the grievant and two members of the Union’s 
Grievance Committee within ten business days to discuss the grievance and attempt to resolve the 
grievance.  A written decision from the Deputy Fire Chief shall be delivered to the grievant, Union, 
and Fire Chief within five business days of the meeting.  If the decision fails to satisfy the grievant 
and/or Union, either may initiate Step 2 of the grievance process. 
 
 

15 
 
Step 2 
 
A grievant and/or Union’s Grievance Committee who does not agree with the Step 1 
response and still feels that they have been grieved, may initiate Step 2 and forward the original 
grievance and written responses from Step 1 to the Fire Chief within ten business days of receipt 
of the written decision from Step 1. 
 
Upon receipt, the Fire Chief shall have five business days to schedule and meet with 
grievant and/or Union’s Grievance Committee to hear the grievance and associated facts to seek a 
resolution.  If a resolution is reached, the decision shall be reduced to writing and signed by all 
parties within ten business days.  If a resolution cannot be reached, the Fire Chief shall deliver a 
decision to the parties involved and Mayor within ten business days.  If the decision fails to satisfy 
the grievant and/or Union’s Grievance Committee, either may initiate Step 3 of the process. 
 
It is understood that nothing shall preclude the Grievance Committee from electing to skip 
Step 1 and begin with presenting a grievance to the Fire Chief if it is deemed that such action is 
warranted by the nature or circumstances of the grievance. 
Step 3 
 
A grievant and/or Union’s Grievance Committee who does not agree to the Step 2 response 
and still feels they have been grieved, may initiate Step 3 and forward all original grievance and 
responses to the Mayor within ten business days of receipt of the Fire Chief’s decision. The Mayor 
and Fire Chief shall meet with all parties involved within ten business days of receipt of the Step 
3 notification. 
 
In attendance at the meeting shall be the Mayor or their designee, the aggrieved party, the 
Union’s Grievance Committee, and at the Union’s option, its legal counsel.  If the grievance is 

16 
 
settled as a result of such meeting, the settlement shall be reduced to writing and signed by the 
parties within five days following the meeting.  If no settlement is reached, the Mayor or their 
designee shall give a written answer to the grievance within five business days following the 
meeting.  The grievance may then be submitted to arbitration by the Union. 
Arbitration 
 
Within five business days from the receipt of the Mayor or their designee’s written answer 
referred to above, the City and the Union shall request in writing that the American Arbitration 
Association shall submit a list of five arbitrators.  Within five business days after receipt of said 
list, one impartial arbitrator shall be selected.  A coin flip shall determine which side will be first 
in striking a name from the list.  The name last remaining shall be the appointed impartial 
arbitrator. 
 
Arbitration shall proceed pursuant to the provisions of the Uniform Arbitration Act.  (Wyo. 
Stat. §§ 1-36-101 through 1-36-119).  The selected arbitrator shall have no right to amend, modify, 
alter, add to, or subtract from the provisions of the Agreement.  The arbitrator shall only consider 
and make a decision with respect to the specific issue or issues submitted and shall have no 
authority to make a decision on any other issue not so submitted to the arbitrator.  In the event the 
arbitrator finds a violation of the terms of the Agreement, the arbitrator shall fashion an appropriate 
remedy.  Any dispute, claim, or grievance arising from or relating to interpretation or application 
of this Agreement shall be submitted to arbitration administered by the American Arbitration 
Association under its Labor Arbitration Rules, unless the parties otherwise have agreed in this 
Section.  The parties further agree to accept the arbitrator’s award as final and binding on them, 
subject to the right of either party to seek judicial review pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §§ 1-36-101, et 

17 
 
seq.  The parties shall each pay half the costs and expenses of the impartial arbitrator.  Should the 
same issue be submitted to arbitration two or more times during the term of this Agreement and 
the same party loses two or more times on that issue, the losing party shall pay all costs and 
expenses of the second or any subsequent impartial arbitration. 

18 
 
SECTION 9.  NON-DISCRIMINATION 
 
Neither the City nor the Union shall discriminate against a Member of the Department by 
any means whatsoever because of race, sex, sexual orientation, gender identity, creed, color, 
national origin, or Union affiliation with I.A.F.F. Local Union No. 279.  The City and the Union 
shall comply with the requirements of Title VII of the Civil Rights Act of 1964, as amended, the 
Fair Employment Practices Act of 1965, as amended (Wyo. Stat. §§ 27-9-101, et seq.), and other 
applicable state and federal laws. 
 

19 
 
SECTION 10.  ANNEXATIONS 
 
The City and the Union agree that should the City annex any new areas the Department 
will provide first response to all calls in all annexed areas with the exception of the F. E. Warren 
Air Force Base. 
 

20 
 
SECTION 11.  VOLUNTARY DEMOTION 
 
Any Member who voluntarily requests a demotion from the Member’s current rank shall 
be demoted to the rank held immediately prior to the Member’s promotion.  The Member shall 
make the request in writing at least thirty days prior to the effective date of the requested demotion.  
The request shall be subject to approval by the Fire Chief.  The Fire Chief may not, prior to the 
expiration of the thirty-day period, appoint another Member to the position from which the 
Member wishes to be demoted.  Upon the effective date, the Member shall return to their previous 
rank and grade. 
 

21 
 
SECTION 12.  UNION REPRESENTATION 
 
In the event any Member of the Department appears before the Deputy Fire Chief, Fire 
Chief, the Mayor or their designee, for work-related disciplinary action, that Member shall have 
the option of having one elected Union representative present.  The person or persons being 
disciplined shall only be called in while on duty.  The elected Union representative shall be granted 
time off without loss of wages. 
 
For the purpose of this Section, the term “work-related disciplinary action” includes, but is 
not limited to, verbal or written reprimands, demotion, suspension, or termination actions that 
become part of the Member’s personnel file. 
 

22 
 
SECTION 13.  MANAGEMENT RIGHTS 
 
Except as otherwise provided in this Agreement, the City shall have the right to direct and 
manage the operations of the Department and its employees, and to maintain the efficiency of the 
Department including: 
 
1. 
The determination of Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department policy, including the right 
to manage the affairs of the Department. 
 
2. 
The right to establish and modify the amount of apparatus in the main or reserve 
fleet. 
 
3. 
The right to direct the members of the Department, including the right to hire, 
promote, discipline, or transfer Members to another shift. 
 
4. 
The collaboration for the safety, health, and property-protection measurers 
provided by the Department for the citizens of Cheyenne. 
 
5. 
The selection and promotion of employees established by the Civil Service 
Commission in its rules and regulations. 
 
6. 
The determination of policy training of employees. 
 
7. 
The establishment, modification, and enforcement of Department rules, 
regulations, and orders. 
 
8. 
The determination of the amount of supervision necessary. 
 
9. 
The transfer of employees from one station to the other. 
 
10. 
The establishment and determination of specialized emergency response teams to 
include roles, responsibilities, and training requirements. 

23 
 
 
11. 
The right to establish job descriptions for each duty position within the department 
as identified in Section 21, Classification of Employment and Basic Wages.  
12.  
The right to staff apparatus and equipment based on community risk analysis needs 
and as identified in Section 2, Definitions and Section 15, Department Staffing.  
13.  
The right to manage organizational risk including, but not limited to financial crisis, 
natural disasters, cyber incidents, global pandemics, and social emergencies with specific written 
policy and departmental continuity of operations. 
 
It is agreed that no conduct or action of the City or Union hereunder shall breach the 
provisions of the Agreement, the Civil Service Rules and Regulations in effect on July 1, 2023, 
and the laws of the State of Wyoming governing Firefighters, and the discretionary power invested 
in the City and the Fire Chief shall not be exercised in an arbitrary or capricious manner. 

24 
 
SECTION 14.  STRIKES 
 
During the term of this Agreement, there shall be no strike, slowdown, suspension, or work 
stoppage by the Members of the Department, nor shall there be any lock-out or work interference 
by the City. 
 

25 
 
SECTION 15.  DEPARTMENT STAFFING 
 
The Department shall maintain a minimum staffing level of one hundred firefighters, 
including the Fire Chief and one Deputy Fire Chief, with a minimum of twenty-nine firefighters 
assigned to each shift. When Department staffing falls below the minimum level of firefighters, it 
shall be brought back to the minimum level as follows: 
 
The Fire Chief shall utilize the current Civil Service Commission eligibility list to begin 
the replacement process for the available openings.  The Fire Chief may elect to: 
1. 
Hire person(s) who have two or more years of documented paid professional 
firefighting experience, with initial training equal to or higher than an acceptable fire 
training academy. Additionally, the potential new hire must submit all continuing 
education completed by them as documented by their current training division for review.  
If the potential new hire meets or exceeds the base minimum training as compared to 
Department standards, the Chief may elect to hire the person(s) and have them begin an 
orientation program and upon completion report to shift work; or 
2. 
Hire personnel and send them through an acceptable fire training academy 
or equivalent program. 
Should a vacancy be filled with a lateral hire, this member shall enter the rank of 
Firefighter. The lateral member’s consecutive years of service from their previous agency 
will determine their step grade but shall not exceed Firefighter Step-1. For example, if a 
lateral hire has two consecutive years of service with another fire department and conforms 
to Section 15 subsection 1 of this CLA, they will be paid at the Firefighter Step-2 rate. The 
credit for time does not apply to, or affect, an individual’s seniority date or service date, 

26 
 
and is therefore not recognized for benefits associated with those dates (i.e., promotional 
time requirements, station picks, vacation selection, seniority points for examinations, 
reduction in force, etc.). This member’s first year of employment shall be of a probationary 
status. The Fire Chief shall have discretion to adjust initial pay step with a rate maximum 
of Firefighter Step-1. 
 
To accommodate fire-training academy schedules, the Department may delay hiring new 
Members until seven days prior to the start of spring and fall academies, while still recognizing 
the importance of minimum staffing levels. 
 
A. 
If a Member separates from service within forty-five days or less of the start of a 
fire training academy program, the City is not required to hire a replacement to attend the 
immediate academy but shall have hired the replacement(s) at least seven days prior to the 
subsequent academy. 
 
B. 
The staffing of the Suppression, Community Risk Reduction (CRR), Support and 
Administration Divisions, excluding the Fire Chief and Deputy Fire Chief, shall be at the minimum 
level of ninety-eight at least seven days prior to the start of the spring and fall academies, and shall 
not fall below the minimum staffing level until another Member separates from service. 
 
C. 
Each new firefighter shall satisfactorily complete an acceptable fire academy 
program or orientation held by either the Department or the Front Range Fire Consortium prior to 
entering the ranks of the Department.  Upon completion of the fire academy, the new Member 
shall be reviewed on a regular basis as set forth in a defined probationary period, approved by the 
Department.  During the probationary period, a Member may be terminated at any time without 
cause. 

27 
 
 
When shift staffing is reduced from vacancies (retirement, separation, or transfer to days), 
the use of E.O.D. time may be restricted to ensure proper shift minimum levels. 
 
D. 
The use of E.O.D. compensatory time is restricted for Platoon Personnel when a 
Member is transferred to a forty-hour week duty assignment or reassigned by the Fire Chief. 
Company Strength 
 
For the safety of the Members and the citizens of Cheyenne, and the effective utilization 
of fire equipment, the Department shall employ engine companies, a ladder company, and a 
Battalion Chief, all without dual assignment and as defined for suppression operations, for a 
minimum staffing of twenty firefighters. The Fire Chief may employ additional resources such as 
an engine, ladder, squad, rescue, specialty apparatus, as the Fire Chief determines as necessary. 
 
All firefighting equipment dispatched to all structural fires shall consist of a minimum of 
sixteen members without an aerial ladder truck and seventeen members with an aerial ladder truck. 
Members on the first alarm, and any alarm thereafter shall be answered at the discretion of the 
Incident Commander. The Incident Commander shall have full authority to request additional 
resources, make appropriate assignments and comply with the 2 in-2 out standard.  
 
In a Mutual Aid request for assistance, the City need only respond with the requested 
equipment.  The on-the-scene Officer in Charge of such equipment may, for safety reasons, request 
additional equipment through the Authority Having Jurisdiction Incident Commander before 
involving the crew at the scene. 

28 
 
SECTION 16.  RESERVE MANPOWER 
 
In the event of an emergency that in the judgment of the Incident Commander necessitates 
additional manpower, the Incident Commander may call in off-duty personnel of the Department.  
The Incident Commander may request mutual aid assistance when justified by the circumstances. 
 

29 
 
SECTION 17.  HOURS OF WORK 
 
The average work week for Platoon Personnel shall be fifty-six hours computed on a 
twenty-four-day basis.  The three-shift system shall be maintained with twenty-four-hour tours of 
duty, and the existing schedule shall be continued, which will include scheduled work time from 
the hours of 0800 to 1700 with one hour off for lunch and one hour for physical fitness training. 
 
Beginning January 7, 2023, the employer shall begin the work shifts per work period as 
follows: the stated work period for purposes of complying with the Fair Labor Standards Act shall 
be twenty-four days.  Each Member shall be assigned to work shifts that total 192 hours of work 
per period.  The assigned schedule shall be a 48/96 shift schedule, three platoon system in which 
employees work two consecutive twenty-four hour shifts for a total of forty-eight hours and have 
ninety-six consecutive hours off. The maximum number of consecutive hours worked by members 
shall be ninety-six hours. 
 
 
Day Personnel will be primarily scheduled to work four 10-hour days (40-hour 
work week) as defined in the definitions.  Shifts may begin at 0700 hours and may end at 1800 
hours. This time may be adjusted to meet the needs of the Fire Department or the Member with 
the approval of the Fire Chief.  The schedule may have one weekday, and shall have Saturday, 
Sunday, and holidays off.  If a holiday falls on a Member’s regularly scheduled day off, the 
Member shall receive the day before the holiday or the next regularly scheduled workday off, as 
determined by the Fire Chief. 

30 
 
SECTION 18.  TRADING SHIFTS 
 
Members shall be permitted to trade shifts or parts of a shift if equal duties can be assumed 
with permission of the Battalion Chief or designee, providing there is no additional cost to the 
City.  No Member shall be permitted to compensate another Member for a shift worked, either in 
money, or in any other form of remuneration.  
 

31 
 
SECTION 19.  INCLEMENT WEATHER CONDITIONS 
 
It is the responsibility of each Member of the Department to make an honest effort to report 
to work as scheduled.  During periods of severe inclement weather, Members are classified as 
essential personnel. 
 
If a Member cannot report for duty as scheduled without an unreasonable risk to their 
safety, the Member shall immediately notify an Officer in Charge at Headquarters by the hours 
specified in the rules and regulations to report the Member’s status.  The following will apply to: 
Platoon Personnel: 
 
The Officer in Charge shall notify the Member who has to be relieved of duty and place 
that Member on holdover until properly relieved. 
 
The affected Member shall be: 
1. 
Granted two hours of administrative leave without penalty to report to duty.  
If a Member cannot arrive safely in the two hours, the Member may request from the Fire 
Chief or Deputy Fire Chief an additional two hours of administrative leave without penalty 
(maximum of four hours administrative leave). 
2. 
If the Member is still unable to safely report to duty by noon, they may 
obtain an emergency time swap for the period needed to report to duty or utilize accrued 
vacation or E.O.D. time in hour increments until they are able to report to duty. 
Day Personnel: 
 
Members who work a forty-hour work week shall be required to observe the following: 
If the City remains open and a Day Personnel Member cannot safely report for duty, 
the affected Member will contact their appropriate supervisor to report the Member’s 

32 
 
status.  The Member affected by inclement weather will be allowed to flex the needed time 
to safely report to duty or utilize accrued vacation or E.O.D. time on an hour for hour basis 
for time absent until they are able to safely report to duty. 
If City offices are closed, or a delayed start is declared by the Mayor of the City of 
Cheyenne, Day Personnel will be granted time off without penalty. 
In the event of a delayed start, Day Personnel will be required to report for duty at 
the designated time or will be allowed to flex the needed time to safely report to duty or 
utilize accrued vacation or E.O.D. time on an hour for hour basis for time absent until they 
are able to safely report to duty. 
The Department will not charge accrued vacation or E.O.D. compensatory time for 
time missed in the event a Day Personnel Member is directed by the Fire Chief or their 
designee to leave work early due to inclement weather. 
 

33 
 
SECTION 20.  HOLIDAYS 
 
The following are the recognized paid holidays for this contract period for platoon 
personnel only. Day personnel shall be off on days that are congruent with the City of Cheyenne 
holiday schedule, with full pay.  
 
New Year’s Day 
 
Martin Luther King, Jr., Day 
 
President’s Day 
 
Memorial Day 
 
Juneteenth National Independence Day 
 
July 4th 
 
One-half day for Cheyenne Day 
 
Labor Day 
 
Columbus Day  
 
Veteran’s Day 
 
Thanksgiving Day 
 
Christmas Day 
 
Department Members shall be granted a choice of either E.O.D. compensatory time off for 
the actual number of hours worked during the holiday or paid compensatory wages at their regular 
base rate for that shift on the holiday hours worked and shall not be deprived of and shall receive 
in addition to their regular base pay for the day. 
 
 
The Department shall refrain from scheduling activities on the holidays designated 
herein. 

34

SECTION 22.  OVERTIME 
Overtime rates shall be paid on the following work: 
 
A. 
Holiday overtime. 
 
B. 
Ordered duty overtime and hold over overtime. 
 
C. 
Overtime work in excess of 182 hours per twenty-four-day work period. 
 
When computing total hours in a work period, Members shall be given hour for hour credit 
for the following paid leave: 
 
A. 
Bereavement leave. 
 
B. 
Leave due to injury on-duty. 
 
C. 
E.O.D. 
 
D. 
Vacations. 
 
E.     
Military 
 
All hours worked up to and including 182 hours in a twenty-four-day work period shall be 
compensated at the base rate for the classification that the Member is assigned to as defined in 
Section 21, Classification of Employment and Basic Wages, and will be inclusive of all regularly 
scheduled education, specialty, and longevity pays.  All other remuneration to the Member such 
as pay for vacation, sick, and holidays shall not be considered as regularly scheduled pay for the 
purposes of this Section. 
 
All hours worked in a twenty-four-day work period in excess of 182 hours will be paid an 
additional one and one-half times the base rate as defined in Section 21, Classification of 
Employment and Basic Wages, and will be inclusive of all regularly scheduled education, 
specialty, and longevity pays.  All other remuneration to the Member such as pay for vacation, 

41 
 
sick, and holidays shall not be considered as regularly scheduled pay for the purposes of this 
Section. 
 
Overtime shall be paid at the rate of one and one-half times the individual’s base rate, and 
will be inclusive of all regularly scheduled education, specialty, and longevity pays, or the base 
rate of the position they are filling, and will be inclusive of all regularly scheduled education, 
specialty, and longevity pays, whichever is higher.  All other remuneration to the Member such as 
pay for vacation, sick, and holidays shall not be considered as regularly scheduled pay for the 
purposes of this Section.  All personnel shall be granted their choice of overtime pay or E.O.D. 
compensatory time off at the rate of one and one-half hours off for each hour worked. 
 
If overtime work is required to cover a vacancy in any position, such work shall be offered 
to Department Members from a roster which includes all Members.  The shift roster shall be 
maintained by the Union and monitored by the Fire Chief and/or their designees, and such work 
will be offered in as equitable a manner as possible.  Any position filled pursuant to this Section 
shall be paid at the Member’s base rate or the rate for the position they are filling, whichever is 
higher. 
 
Overtime work on a holiday shall be paid at an additional two times the base rate for the 
hours that are on the holiday. 
 
All Daytime positions shall be filled at the discretion of the Fire Chief. 
 
Mandatory call out pay shall be two hours minimum.  After the two hours minimum, one 
to thirty minutes shall be compensated for as one-half hour, and thirty-one to sixty minutes shall 
be compensated for as one hour. 
 

42 
 
 
The maximum accrual for E.O.D. compensatory time will be 480 hours.  This maximum 
accrual applies to any balance of E.O.D. compensatory time including, but not limited to, amount 
earned from committee work, special projects or overtime, sick leave bonus.  Any E.O.D. 
compensatory time over 480 hours must be taken during the calendar year in which it was earned.  
No Member will be allowed to carry over a balance of E.O.D. compensatory time greater than 480 
hours on December 31.  The use of E.O.D. compensatory time cannot create an overtime situation.  
Members hired after July 1, 2019, shall be capped at a maximum of 240 hours of E.O.D. 
When Day Personnel are working in an overtime capacity on a platoon shift, their base 
rate, education, specialty, EMS, and longevity pays shall be converted to a 56-hour pay rate.  

43 
 
SECTION 23.  WORKING IN A HIGHER JOB RANK 
A. In the event a Platoon Member promoted above the rank of Firefighter is absent from 
their normally scheduled shift, a qualified Member will assume those duties for the 
duration of the absence. The assigned Member shall receive all regularly scheduled 
base, education, specialty, and longevity pays that the Member is entitled to. Members 
certified to act shall be paid in accordance with Section 21, “Acting Certification Pay”. 
Daytime Members are excluded from Acting Certification Pay. Members are eligible 
for one Acting Certification Pay regardless of the number of certifications the member 
obtains. At the discretion of the Fire Chief, should a member act in a higher job rank 
for more than three consecutive months, that member shall be paid the Step 2 rate of 
said rank. If that member is not qualified to act beyond said rank, they will not qualify 
for acting certification pay while receiving the Step 2 rate.  
B. When filling an absence through the acting mechanism, preference may be given to 
retain the acting Member at the station in which they have been assigned or chosen. 
Additionally, a Member’s ranking on the “eligibility list” may be taken into account to 
provide for said Member to act more frequently. Final acting placement for daily 
operations will be at the discretion of the corresponding shift’s battalion chief. 
C. Except in cases of overtime, the Member(s) assigned to fill said position(s) shall be 
from the same shift. In the event there are no Members from the same shift available to 
act, the absence shall be covered by a qualified Member using the overtime mechanism. 
D. Certification eligibility: 
a. Members previously certified or who have completed departmental training and 
testing shall be grandfathered. 

44 
 
b. All members must complete departmental training and testing as approved by the 
Fire Chief. The Fire Chief shall establish a career progression pathway for all 
Members. Formal acting academies for Engineer, Lieutenant, and Battalion Chief 
shall be available to those qualified members at a minimum of every two years. 
c. Firefighters assigned to act in the capacity of Engineer must have completed two 
years of service to the Department. 
d. Firefighters assigned to act in the capacity of Lieutenant must have completed four 
years of service to the Department. 
e. Engineers assigned to act in the capacity of Lieutenant must have completed four 
years of service to the Department. 
f. Lieutenants assigned to act in the capacity of Battalion Chief must have completed 
eight years of service to the Department.   
g. Any Member may choose to remove themselves from the acting pool, said Member 
shall not receive any corresponding acting pay referenced in Section 21. If the 
Member chooses to reinstate their name into the acting pool, their qualifications and 
eligibility will be subject to review and approval of their departmental training and 
testing as approved by the Fire Chief. The frequency and circumstances surrounding 
the Member’s choice to remove or reinstate themselves may be considered by the 
Fire Chief when reviewing and approving the Member’s reinstatement.  
E. In the event a Member occupying a Day Personnel position is absent due to illness, 
injury, disability, or other cause and it is expected that the Member will resume the 
performance of the duties of the position, the Fire Chief may assign another Member 
to the position as an actor.  If the Fire Chief chooses to assign an actor, the Fire Chief 
shall assign the highest-ranking Member on the eligibility list for the position, 
established by the Civil Service Commission in its Rules and Regulations, who is 
willing to serve as an actor.  If the eligibility list for the position has become exhausted 
pursuant to Chapter I, Section 3.s., of the Civil Service Commission Rules and 

45 
 
Regulations, the position shall be offered to Members whose names appear on a roster 
established to fill the position. 
F. All Members on the acting roster for Day Personnel positions shall have four years of 
employment with the Department and the Fire Chief may require possession of a Class 
B driver’s license.  In the absence of an established acting roster, the Fire Chief may 
assign a Member to fill the position by temporary assignment.  Any Member shall be 
placed on or removed from a roster by notifying the Fire Chief in writing. 
G. Light duty, as defined in Section 27 of this Agreement, will not be considered working 
in a higher job rank. 
H. A temporary assignment, if assigned will adhere to Section 2 of this agreement, with 
the following conditions: 
a. If an assigned Member’s work schedule is modified, Section 24 of this agreement 
will be followed to convert pay and benefit hours for the duration of the assignment.  
b. A Member shall not be converted to a lower rank or classification in the event of a 
temporary assignment. 
c. The Member will continue to receive all specialty, education, longevity, acting and 
other pays the Member is entitled to for the duration of the assignment. 

46 
 
SECTION 24.  CONVERSION OF BENEFIT HOURS 
 
A. 
Members who move from a Platoon Personnel rank or position to a Day Personnel 
rank or position, and Members who move from a Day Personnel rank or position to a Platoon 
Personnel rank or position, pursuant to promotion, transfer, temporary assignment, or Light Duty 
will be subject to benefit conversion.  The benefit conversion will be calculated under the following 
formulas: 
1. 
Platoon to Day:  Divide the number of hours worked while on Days divided 
by hours worked on Platoon per month.  Conversion factor shall be 0.712 
(173.333/243.333) = 0.712. 
Example:  A Platoon Personnel Member has accrued 200 hours times 0.712 for a 
total of 142.4 hours (200 X 0.712) = 142.4. 
2. 
Day to Platoon:  Divide the number of hours worked while on Platoon 
divided by hours worked on Days.  Conversion factor shall be 1.403 (243.333/173.333) = 
1.403. 
Example:  A Day Personnel Member has accrued 100 hours times 1.403 for a total 
of 140.3 hours (100 X 1.403) = 140.3.   
 
B. 
These calculations shall apply to conversions of vacation and sick hours. 
 
C. 
Members with less than thirty days in assignment shall not be covered. 

47 
 
SECTION 25.  WELLNESS/FITNESS PROGRAM 
 
The City agrees to provide an annual physical for all Members per National Fire Protection 
Association 1582 guidelines.  In addition, specialty team members will receive any additional 
testing as required by the standard. 
 
Furthermore, the City and the Union agree to adopt and promote the International 
Association of Fire Chief’s International Association of Fire Fighters Peer Fitness and Wellness 
Program. 
 
This benefit does not constitute a prevailing right. 
 

48 
 
SECTION 26.  VACATIONS/E.O.D. 
 
A. 
Annual vacations shall be determined for the period from January 1 to December 
31 of each year based on the years of service completed prior to January 1 of each year. 
 
B. 
All vacation time shall be accrued. 
 
C. 
All rosters shall be made up prior to January 1 of each year in which the vacations 
are to be taken. 
 
D. 
All Platoon Personnel shall choose their vacations on their respective shift, 
according to their seniority on the job. 
 
E. 
A separate vacation roster shall be maintained for each shift. 
 
F. 
A maximum of five slots may be available at any time, four vacation or E.O.D. 
slots and one E.O.D. only slot, except for override vacations due to transfers or approvals from the 
Fire Chief. 
 
G. 
Vacations shall be picked by seniority of the Shift.  The Senior Member shall select 
complete sets until they are either unable to select more or elects to stop their pick.  The next Senior 
Member shall then select complete sets until they are either unable to select more or elects to stop 
their pick. The sequence shall continue by seniority until the entire Shift has picked.  The sequence 
shall start over by seniority and continue until all Members have exhausted their accrued days 
and/or non-complete sets (individual shifts) from available dates. Requests for changes to 
vacations or E.O.D. shall be made to the Battalion Chief of the Shift and shall be awarded on a 
first come first serve basis on December 1 at 0800. 

49 
 
 
H. 
If, after sick call, there are additional openings that do not affect minimum staffing, 
Members may request “Day of Vacation or E.O.D.” as long as the additional slots do not create 
overtime.  Openings shall be awarded on a first come first serve basis. 
 
I. 
Day Personnel shall be carried on a separate vacation roster from the Platoon 
Personnel, and they may split vacations. 
 
J. 
During the term of this Agreement, a Member’s vacation or balance thereof shall 
be rescheduled into available vacation slots due to promotion and demotion.  If a Member is 
involuntarily moved, that Member retains their slot at their new assignment.  In the event a Member 
is involuntarily moved, and a slot is vacated by any personnel in the four-member group, that slot 
shall be considered vacated and the maximum of four Members off will take effect.  In the event 
there are no vacation slots available, only then will a Member be allowed to carry their vacation 
beyond the maximum four persons off at any one time. 
 
K. 
All Platoon Personnel shall be entitled to a vacation with full pay and all benefits 
as follows: 
After successful completion of the fire academy, a pro-rated amount of 24-hour shifts at 
.8333 shifts per each full month of continuous service to be taken in the following year; 
After one year of completed service, ten 24-hour regularly assigned tours of duty; 
After five years of completed service, eleven 24-hour regularly assigned tours of duty; 
After ten years of completed service, twelve 24-hour regularly assigned tours of duty; 
After fifteen years of completed service, thirteen 24-hour regularly assigned tours of duty; 
After twenty years of completed service, fourteen 24-hour regularly assigned tours of duty; 

50 
 
After twenty-five years of completed service, fifteen 24-hour regularly assigned tours of 
duty; 
After thirty years of completed service, sixteen 24-hour regularly assigned tours of duty. 
All platoon vacation accrued shall be scheduled and used each calendar year, with no 
carryover of days allowed, unless a member is unable due to injury or illness. 
 
L. 
All Day Personnel shall be entitled to a vacation with full pay and all benefits as 
follows: 
 
After one year of completed service, 120 hours; 
 
After five years of completed service, 140 hours; 
 
After ten years of completed service, 170 hours; 
 
After fifteen years of completed service, 190 hours; 
 
After twenty years of completed service, 220 hours; 
 
After twenty-five years of completed service, 240 hours; 
 
After thirty years of completed service, 260 hours. 
Day Personnel assigned after July 1, 2023, shall not carry over more than 240 hours of 
vacation from one calendar year to the next.  
 

51 
 
SECTION 27.  SICK LEAVE 
 
All Members of the Department shall earn accumulative sick leave at the rate of one shift 
for each full month completed until a maximum of 1,440 hours for platoon and 1,025 hours for 
day personnel have been accrued.  All sick leave not in excess of the maximum allowable, which 
has been accrued by Members of the Department at the date of this Agreement, shall be considered 
to be vested and shall continue in effect.  At the time of separation from the Department, Members 
with at least four years of continuous service shall be paid fifty percent of their accrued sick leave 
up to a maximum of 240 hours at their regular base rate computed on 2,080 annual hours not to 
exceed thirty 8-hour shifts.  Sick leave is hereby defined to mean absence from post of duty of a 
Member because of personal illness or injury of the Member.  Sick leave may be used for 
attendance upon an immediate family member due to illness, injury, or accident up to a maximum 
of three sick leave shifts per year. 
 
Sick leave shall not be considered as a privilege which a Member may use at their discretion 
but shall be allowed only in case of necessity and actual sickness or disability of the Member or 
immediate family member. 
 
A. 
Beginning January 1, 2014, all Members who take ninety-six hours or less of sick 
leave for a calendar year shall be paid a cash bonus or E.O.D. compensatory time as set forth 
herein. 
 
B. 
All Members shall be eligible for up to ninety-six hours of pay or E.O.D. 
compensatory time when they use less than ninety-six hours of sick leave in a calendar year.  For 
every hour of sick leave used, the pay or E.O.D. compensatory time shall be reduced by one hour 

52 
 
from the possible ninety-six hours.  Members shall only be able to take up to forty-eight hours of 
pay.  All hours earned over forty-eight shall be taken as E.O.D. compensatory time. 
 
C. 
If the Member takes any of the first forty-eight hours as E.O.D. compensatory time, 
up to forty-eight of those hours can be banked for future use.  All of the hours earned above forty-
eight shall be used on or before December 31 of the year in which they were awarded or they will 
be lost. 
 
D. 
This benefit will be prorated for new Members who have been employed for more 
than six months but less than one year. 
 
E. 
After two consecutive working shifts off-duty from illness or injury, a doctor’s 
certificate of examination shall be required if requested by the Fire Chief covering the period of 
sickness or disability.  In addition to the above, a Member may be required to provide a doctor’s 
certificate of examination in the event sick leave is taken for the shift immediately preceding or 
following a vacation period.  Any examination as ordered shall be paid for by the City.  Upon 
presentation of satisfactory information, the Fire Chief may extend the sick leave or waive the 
doctor’s excuse requirement.  If an extension is refused, an appeal may be made to the Mayor. 
 
F. 
Members may, at any time during the calendar year, donate up to one hundred 
twenty hours of their unused sick leave into the Department Sick Leave Bank or the City Sick 
Leave Bank.  A maximum amount of six hundred 24-hour shifts will be accumulated in the 
Department Sick Leave Bank.  Members will be allowed to use sick leave from the Department 
Sick Leave Bank only when all personal sick leave benefits have been used.  Written application 
for the use of the Department Sick Leave Bank must be made to a Sick Leave Bank committee 
consisting of the Union president or their designee, the Fire Chief, and the Mayor or their designee, 

53 
 
within ten business days prior to the date the sick leave is needed.  The committee may, at its 
discretion, request a doctor’s certificate of examination before granting use of the Department Sick 
Leave Bank.  Denial of a sick leave request is not subject to appeal or grievance.  Members will 
continue to accrue benefits during the usage of sick leave from the Department Sick Leave Bank. 
 
G. 
Upon separation of employment with the City, Members may donate the balance 
of uncompensated hours to the City Sick Leave Bank. 
 
H. 
Members who are recuperating from an injury or illness may return to duty to 
perform work (light duty) consistent with their abilities as determined by the Member’s physician.  
The Member shall, upon request, furnish the City with a physician’s statement specifying the type 
of work the Member may perform.  The statement shall specify medically necessary restrictions, 
if any.  The Fire Chief shall place the Member in a position which contributes in a meaningful and 
identifiable way to the goals and needs of the Department. 
 
I. 
Any Member who is injured in the discharge of their duties shall file such reports 
of accident disability or injury, as shall be required to secure the payment of benefits under the 
Wyoming Worker’s Compensation Act to the injured Member.  While injured, said Member shall 
use their accrued sick leave and once exhausted may use the Department Sick Leave Bank until 
return to duty or pension disability is received.  If the Member receives temporary total disability 
payments under the Worker’s Compensation Act, the Member shall transfer those monies to the 
City. 
 
J. 
Claims for medical or hospital charges, drugs, and other compensable medical 
services shall be filed with the Worker’s Compensation Division.  If a Member becomes ill or 

54 
 
disabled prior to their regularly scheduled vacation, the Member and Fire Chief, by mutual 
agreement, shall re-schedule the vacation for said Member into available vacation slots, if possible. 
 
K. 
Sick leave will be charged by the hour, one hour for every hour off.  In the event 
that a Member returns to work after being on disability pension, said Member shall return to the 
rank and position they held previous to the disability without loss of seniority if return to work is 
within eighteen months. 
 
 

55 
 
SECTION 28.  BEREAVEMENT LEAVE 
 
In the case of death within the immediate family of any Member of the Department, such 
Member shall be granted leave with pay. 
 
A Member who is assigned to a Platoon Personnel position may have three shifts of leave 
and a Member assigned to a Day Personnel may have four shifts of leave.  Under extenuating 
circumstances, additional leave may be granted by the Fire Chief. 
 
The Fire Chief may, in the exercise of discretion, grant bereavement leave upon the death 
of a person other than the Member’s immediate family. 
 

56 
 
SECTION 29.  LEAVE OF ABSENCE 
 
Any Member of the Department may be granted a Leave of Absence without pay for a 
period not to exceed three months.  The request for such leave shall be made in writing to the Fire 
Chief not less than ten days before such leave would begin and is subject to the Fire Chief and the 
Mayor’s approval.  The Fire Chief may also place a Member on a leave of absence with pay. 
 
No Member shall be granted a leave of absence to work in another job full time or be newly 
employed and receive benefits from another employer. 
 

57 
 
SECTION 30.  MEDICAL, DENTAL, AND LIFE INSURANCE 
 
Except as provided in this Section, the City shall pay the premium cost for Health, Dental, 
and Life Insurance under “The City Employees’ Insurance Contract,” for those Members desiring 
to obtain such coverage.  If the City offers more than one health insurance plan to City employees, 
Members shall be offered the plan providing more extensive benefits.  Each participant in the City 
health insurance program shall contribute at the same rate as other City employees.  Dental 
insurance coverage shall be provided for the Member.  Dental insurance coverage for the 
Member’s family is available at the Member’s expense. 
 
A. 
Members shall be entitled to insure their dependents under the plans offered by the 
City.  An eligible dependent is defined as a spouse and children up to the age of 26, whether 
married or unmarried.  Dependent children aged 26 or older may be eligible to remain insured if a 
physician certifies that the child cannot support themselves because of a disability which occurred 
before they reached the age of 26. 
 
B. 
Any Member with dependent coverage may add new dependent children at any 
time.  However, the application for coverage must be made within thirty days of the birth, 
placement, or adoption of the child. 
 
C. 
The Union shall have the right to appoint one Member to the City Employee’s 
Health Insurance Committee. 
 
The City shall offer to all Members the option of participating in a flexible health benefits 
plan which qualifies as a “cafeteria plan” within the meaning of Section 125 of the Internal 
Revenue Service Code. 
 

58 
 
 
Whenever any Member’s yearly base salary entitles said Member to a higher amount of 
Life Insurance, such Life Insurance increase shall be effective July 1 of each year with a maximum 
of $20,000.00. 
 
The City shall offer Members fifty years of age or older who elect to retire the option of 
maintaining the current health insurance policy at the single rate.  The City shall pay a maximum 
of $300.00 per month toward the single coverage.  The policy shall remain in effect until the 
Member attains the age of eligibility for Medicare or becomes eligible for coverage through 
another employer.  Members hired after July 1, 2013, shall not be eligible for this provision. 
 
If a Member becomes permanently disabled, as defined by the State of Wyoming Worker’s 
Compensation Division, in the line of duty, or dies due to injury or illness incurred in the line of 
duty, said Member and/or their eligible dependents shall be offered health insurance at the expense 
of the City.  The City shall pay for health insurance for the disabled Member.  Said coverage will 
remain in effect until the Member and their eligible dependents become eligible for coverage 
through another employer or become eligible for Medicare.  Coverage shall be equal to or exceed 
the health insurance available to other Members.  Subject to the conditions in this Section, this 
coverage shall be offered the remainder of said Member’s life, and to the dependents of said 
Member as long as they meet the qualifications of a dependent. 
 
Eligible dependents are defined as: 
 
A. 
The spouse, as long as he or she does not remarry. 
 
B. 
Children up to the age of 26, whether married or unmarried. 

59 
 
 
C. 
Dependent children age 26 or older may be eligible to remain insured if a physician 
certifies that the child cannot support themselves because of a disability which occurred before 
they reached the age of 26. 
 
The City of Cheyenne may from time to time require verification of permanent disability 
of Member, of employment, or of a dependent’s age or disability. 
 

60 
 
SECTION 31.  UNIFORMS 
 
A. 
Uniform items and their compliance will be agreed upon between the Fire Chief 
and the Union so as to provide the best options for the Members, while being fiscally responsible 
and assuring for the safety of the Members. 
 
B. 
The City agrees to fund uniform purchases for all Members in an amount of Five 
Hundred Fifty Dollars ($550.00) per Member per year.  The offered uniform items will include, 
but not be limited to, American National Standard Institute (ANSI) compliant work boots, station 
pants, long and short sleeve dress shirts, polo shirt, a tie, Job Shirt sweatshirts, sweatpants, workout 
shorts, coat/jacket, cotton T-shirts (blue, red and white), belts, ball cap, winter cap and formal 
Class A dress uniform and alterations 
 
C. 
A set selection of items for new recruits will be agreed upon between the Fire Chief 
and the Union, or its designee.  Members are responsible for the reasonable care of their uniforms. 
 
D. 
The Department shall purchase each member a Class A dress uniform upon 
completion of their fifth year of service. 
 

61 
 
SECTION 32.  PERSONAL PROTECTIVE EQUIPMENT 
 
The City will furnish and maintain for each Member serviceable structural firefighting 
turnout gear and equipment consisting of one pair of boots, one pair of trousers that includes liners 
and suspenders, one coat that includes liner, one helmet that includes liner, one pair of gloves and 
a flash hood, and one pair of extrication gloves, as needed.  The City will provide coat lights on 
the truck. 
The aforementioned turnout gear shall be NFPA 1971 compliant.  A second set of turnout 
gear consisting of trouser and coat, a second pair of structural firefighting gloves, and a second 
protective hood shall be provided by the City to all platoon members in order to reduce a Member’s 
exposure to carcinogens after a fire.  Should the Department absorb new operations, the 
Department and the Union will agree upon proper equipment to be issued to each Member.  
Members are responsible for the reasonable care of their equipment. 
 

62 
 
SECTION 33.  UNION DUES 
 
Upon written authorization by a Member of the Department, the City agrees, at no cost to 
the Union or Member, to deduct the sum certified as Union Dues, and the sum certified as PAC 
fund contribution, if any; and deliver the amount collected from each to the Union treasurer in 
their respective amounts.  If a Member does not have a check coming, or the check is not large 
enough to satisfy the sum certified, no collection shall be made from said Member for that month. 
 

63 
 
SECTION 34.  MILEAGE ALLOWANCE 
 
If a Member has arrived at their assigned station, and is reassigned to duty at another 
station, said Member shall receive a mileage allowance consistent with City policy for that transfer 
and any other transfers during that shift.  The mileage between stations shall be determined by the 
Fire Chief. 
 

64 
 
SECTION 35.  RESIDENCY 
 
Members will be required to be a resident and be domiciled within a sixty-mile radius of 
the Cheyenne Fire Rescue Headquarters Station 1, located at 716 West 19th Street, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming.  As used herein, a resident is defined as a person who is domiciled and physically 
resides within the above-described area with the present intent to remain there during the terms 
established above.  Intent may be established by the Member’s physical presence, which is 
ongoing, not temporary.  A postal box alone is not sufficient to establish residency.  Violation of 
this Section may be grounds for termination.  Residency requirements must be met within three 
months of the Member’s date of hire or six months within the discretion of the Fire Chief. 
 

65 
 
SECTION 36.  TIME OFF FOR UNION BUSINESS 
 
A maximum of four Members representing the bargaining agent shall be excused with pay 
to attend grievance hearings pursuant to Section 8, and/or the contract negotiations and labor 
management meetings, provided they are attended during their regular working hours. 
 
 
 

66 
 
SECTION 37.  UNION USE OF CITY FACILITIES 
 
The fire station meeting facilities shall be available for Union meetings.  Attendance at 
such meetings by Members of the Union shall be only during off-duty hours, except for those 
Members who are on-duty at the station where such meeting is held may attend after 5:00 p.m.  
Under no circumstance shall the readiness of the Department be compromised during any meeting. 
 

67 
 
SECTION 38.  ALCOHOL AND CONTROLLED SUBSTANCE POLICY 
 
The City and the Union shall abide by the current City of Cheyenne Alcohol and Substance 
Policy. 
 

68 
 
SECTION 39.  FAMILY AND MEDICAL LEAVE POLICY 
 
The City and the Union shall abide by the provisions of the Family and Medical Leave Act. 
 

69 
 
SECTION 40.  PREVAILING RIGHTS 
 
Existing working conditions not specifically covered by this Agreement, but established 
through past practice, shall continue in full force and effect and shall not be altered during the life 
of this Agreement except through the mutual concurrence of the parties, except the City may 
properly and equitably exercise its right to make reasonable and prudent changes in operations 
designed to protect the City from liability or improve the service provided by the Department. 
 

70 
 
SECTION 41.  SAFETY AND HEALTH 
 
The City and the Union shall cooperate fully on all matters concerning the safety and health 
of Members of the Department, and the safety, proper maintenance, and repairs of Department 
equipment and buildings. 
 
To ensure that these matters are taken care of in a timely manner, the Fire Chief and the 
Union shall meet within ten days after receiving written notification of the safety and health matter.  
The Fire Chief will notify and update the Mayor of the matter.  If a resolution has not been agreed 
upon within ten days, the Union shall meet with the Mayor and the Fire Chief. 
 

71 
 
SECTION 42.  LONGEVITY PAY 
 
Each Member who has completed five years of employment with the Department shall 
receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base hourly 
rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$0.2796 
Day Personnel  
 
$0.3925 
 
Each Member who has completed eight years of employment with the Department shall 
receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base hourly 
rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$0.3962 
Day Personnel  
 
$0.5562 
 
Each Member who has completed eleven years of employment with the Department shall 
receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base hourly 
rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$0.5126 
Day Personnel  
 
$0.7196 
 
Each Member who has completed fourteen years of employment with the Department shall 
receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base hourly 
rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$0.6290 
Day Personnel  
 
$0.8830 
 

72 
 
 
Each Member who has completed seventeen years of employment with the Department 
shall receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base 
hourly rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$0.7457 
Day Personnel  
 
$1.0468 
 
Each Member who has completed twenty years of employment with the Department shall 
receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base hourly 
rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$0.8621 
Day Personnel  
 
$1.2102 
 
Each Member who has completed twenty-five years of employment with the Department 
shall receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base 
hourly rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$1.0951 
Day Personnel  
 
$1.5373 
 
Longevity raises shall be effective the first day of the month following the Member’s 
anniversary date of employment except for those Members who began their employment on the 
first day of the month. 
 

73 
 
SECTION 43.  LABOR/MANAGEMENT RELATIONS 
 
There shall be a labor/management committee consisting of up to six Union representatives 
and six City representatives.  The committee shall meet at least quarterly to discuss all matters of 
mutual concern.  Either party may request additional meetings in the quarter as needed.  The 
committee shall have the authority to make recommendations to the Union and the City.  Any 
recommendation by the committee may be rejected by the Union or the City during negotiations. 
 
The Fire Chief and Union shall meet regularly to discuss departmental matters and continue 
to improve working relationships. 
 

74 
 
 
The 2024-2025 Collective Labor Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
and the Cheyenne Firefighters Local Union No. 279, International Association of Firefighters, is 
signed this ________ day of _____________________, 2024.
